Well, I guess... I came in because for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. Like, usually after my shift, I'll grab something to eat, but lately even the foods I usually really like just sit there on the plate while I force down a few bites. 

I work surveillance over at the casino, you know? Night shift. Staring at the camera monitors for hours. It gets pretty wild sometimes. Just the other night we had this guy trying to pull a fast one at the blackjack tables—kept trying to slide his chips when the dealer looked away. We had to send the floor guys down to tap him on the shoulder. You see all kinds of crazy characters in this job, let me tell you, especially around 3 AM. But anyway, yeah, usually I'd hit up the diner after dealing with all that, get a big burger or something to unwind, but now... I just stare at it. I don't really know why.

Yeah, I guess so. It's like... most days I feel like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around with me. Like even when I’m home with my family, or just sitting in the break room at work, I’m just not really there, you know? 

Speaking of the break room, they actually just swapped out all our vending machines last week. Management sent down this memo about a "wellness initiative," so they took out all the good stuff—the jalapeño chips, the decent candy bars—and put in all this organic stuff that tastes like cardboard. My buddy Dave, he works the floor, he was so mad he practically kicked the glass in when he saw it. Dave's a hothead though, always has been. He got into a screaming match with one of the pit bosses last month over a scheduling thing... they almost fired him on the spot. He's got three kids so he can't really afford to lose the job, especially with his truck being in the shop. The transmission blew out on him right on the interstate.

Well, yeah, actually. I used to really look forward to my morning runs. Usually I'd get home from my shift, change, and just hit the pavement before the sun was all the way up. The neighborhood is so quiet then. Except for Mrs. Gable's dog down the street, that little terrier mix. Thing barks at absolutely everything. The mailman, a leaf blowing by, its own shadow... I swear, she just leaves it out in the yard all morning while she watches her shows. I remember one time it got loose and chased this poor squirrel all the way up a telephone pole, and the fire department actually had to come out because the dog somehow got stuck on the lower rung. Took them like an hour to get him down, and Mrs. Gable was just out there in her bathrobe yelling at the firemen. 

But, uh, anyway... yeah, I used to love running, and playing my guitar too, or even just cooking dinner for the family. But lately... I don't know, I keep skipping all of it. It just feels like a chore, you know? Like I just can't get myself to care about doing any of it anymore.

I'd say... probably about three weeks now? Yeah, it started right around the time the casino decided to reorganize our whole security operation and changed up my assignments. 

They brought in this new director, some hotshot from Atlantic City, and the first thing he does is move me off the main floor pit cameras. Now I'm stuck watching the baccarat tables in the high-roller room. It is so dead in there, man. You just watch these guys in expensive suits sip scotch for hours. Actually, one guy fell asleep right at the table last Tuesday. Just face-planted right into his chip stack. We had to call the EMTs because the pit boss thought he was having a heart attack, but nope, the guy had just knocked back way too many complimentary martinis. The paramedics were so annoyed when they got up there. The tall one, Greg I think his name is, he actually comes into the casino on his days off just for the buffet. He swears our crab legs are the best in the city, but honestly, I think they taste like rubber. I don't know how anyone eats them, they just sit under those heat lamps all night. 

But, um... yeah. About three weeks of feeling like this, ever since they switched up my schedule.

I don't really know, honestly. I've tried to think of something, but nothing really comes to mind right now. I mean, my wife keeps telling me I should try watching those old Westerns I used to like. We have this whole box set of John Wayne movies in the basement. 

My father-in-law gave it to us before he moved down to Florida. He bought this little condo right on the water in Boca. Swears he's gonna spend his whole retirement fishing, but the guy gets seasick just looking at a boat, I swear. We went out on a charter with him once, a few years back, and he spent the whole four hours hanging over the railing. The captain felt so bad he gave us a discount, which was nice, but the smell of the bait... man, I can't even look at shrimp anymore without my stomach turning. They use this frozen chum that just bakes in the sun on the deck. 

But, uh, no, I haven't really found anything that I actually enjoy doing right now. Just kind of... existing, I guess.

Took them like an hour to get him down, and Mrs. Gable was just out there in her bathrobe yelling at the firemen. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... yeah, I used to love running, and playing my guitar too, or even just cooking dinner for the family. But lately... I don't know, I keep skipping all of it. It just feels like a chore, you know? Like I just can't get myself to care about doing any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, flat feeling?\n\nPatient: I'd say... probably about three weeks now? Yeah, it started right around the time the casino decided to reorganize our whole security operation and changed up my assignments. \n\nThey brought in this new director, some hotshot from Atlantic City, and the first thing he does is move me off the main floor pit cameras. Now I'm stuck watching the baccarat tables in the high-roller room. It is so dead in there, man. You just watch these guys in expensive suits sip scotch for hours. Actually, one guy fell asleep right at the table last Tuesday. Just face-planted right into his chip stack. Swears he's gonna spend his whole retirement fishing, but the guy gets seasick just looking at a boat, I swear. Worthless? No, not really. I mean, I don't feel guilty about anything either. I just... I don't know, I just show up and do my job. Well, I try to, anyway. It's hard to feel like you're doing anything important when you're just staring at monitors all day. 

Especially since they upgraded the whole camera system last year. They put in these new 4K lenses, right? Supposed to be top of the line. My sleep? No, not really. I'm sleeping about the same as I always do, I guess. I mean, I try to get my usual hours in. It's been a little loud during the day sometimes because the guy next door decided he's a mechanic all of a sudden. He bought this completely rusted-out '98 Honda Civic and he's been revving the engine in his driveway. Thinks he's gonna fix it up and flip it, but the thing doesn't even have a front bumper. 

His wife actually came over to apologize about the noise on Sunday and brought us a plate of cookies. Snickerdoodles, I think? But man, they were hard as rocks. I didn't want to be rude to her, so I took them, but I ended up tossing a couple to that stray cat that hangs around our back porch. Even the cat wouldn't eat them. Just sniffed 'em and walked away. Smart cat, honestly. My wife calls him Barnaby because he's got these crazy long white whiskers that make him look like a grumpy old man. We bought some actual cat food for him, but he only likes the seafood flavor. If you put the chicken one out, he'll just sit there and stare at you through the sliding glass door until you change it. 

But, um... what was the question? Oh, sleep. Yeah, no changes with my sleep. Just the same old routine.

He bought this completely rusted-out '98 Honda Civic and he's been revving the engine in his driveway. Thinks he's gonna fix it up and flip it, but the thing doesn't even have a front bumper. \n\nHis wife actually came over to apologize about the noise on Sunday and brought us a plate of cookies. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Small pleasures... I don't know. Not really. I used to look forward to my morning runs, you know? And I'd play my guitar in the evenings, or make a nice dinner for us. But lately, I just... I keep skipping all of it. It all just feels like chores now, stuff I can't even get myself to care about. 

I actually haven't touched the guitar in weeks. It's just sitting there on the stand in the living room collecting dust. It's an acoustic, a Martin. My dad bought it for me when I graduated high school. He drove all the way out to this little vintage shop in Pennsylvania to get it. The guy who ran the shop was this old hippie who claimed he used to tune guitars for the Grateful Dead back in the seventies. My dad said the guy had hair down to his waist and smelled like patchouli and stale beer. He kept trying to sell my dad these handmade leather straps with peace signs carved into them. 

Dad ended up buying one just to get out of there, but he never used it. It's still in the original plastic bag in my closet somewhere. I think my wife almost threw it out when we were spring cleaning last year. She gets on these kicks where she wants to throw everything away. She read this book by that Japanese lady about folding your socks and only keeping things that spark joy or whatever. Next thing I know, she's trying to throw out my favorite coffee mug just because it has a chip in the handle. I've had that mug for ten years. 

But yeah, the running and the guitar and cooking... I just don't care about any of it right now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Thoughts of death? Um... I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything. I don't have a plan. But lately, I just... I keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up, you know? Just so I wouldn't have to keep pushing through all this everyday. 

It reminds me of this time I had the flu a couple years ago. It was that really bad strain that was going around. My whole body ached, and I slept for like three days straight. My wife had to bring me soup in bed, but I couldn't even eat it. It was this chicken noodle soup from that diner down the street, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing? The "E" has been burnt out for months so it just says "DIN R". The owner is this Greek guy named Nick. He makes the best gyro in town, hands down. The tzatziki sauce is incredible. He told me once that the secret is grating the cucumber and squeezing all the water out before you mix it into the yogurt. If you don't squeeze the water out, it gets all runny and ruins the pita bread. Nobody likes soggy pita bread. I actually tried making it at home once, but I couldn't find the right kind of dill at the grocery store. They only had that dried stuff in the little plastic containers, and Nick says you have to use fresh dill or it just tastes like dust. 

But, uh... yeah. I don't want to kill myself or anything. Just... wouldn't mind if I didn't wake up.

Well, like I was saying a minute ago about the cooking and the guitar and stuff... I actually tried to force myself to make a nice dinner for us just this past Friday. I figured, you know, I used to really look forward to doing it, so maybe if I just start chopping vegetables, I'll enjoy it again. But I was just standing in the kitchen, and it just felt like this massive chore. I couldn't get myself to care at all about how it was going to turn out. 

I was trying to dice an onion, actually. And I was using this new knife sharpener I bought off Amazon because my old chef's knife couldn't even slice a tomato without completely squishing it. You know how frustrating that is? It ruins the whole sandwich. Anyway, the sharpener was supposed to be this heavy-duty tungsten carbide thing. The reviews said it was used by professional butchers, but the delivery guy left the package at the wrong apartment. He left it at 4B instead of 4D. 

The guy in 4B is this weird dude who actually works at the casino with me. I'm usually up in the surveillance room watching the cameras, making sure nobody's capping bets or whatever, but he's a floor manager down in the pit. He always wears these incredibly shiny dress shoes. Like, mirror-finish. I have no idea how he keeps them that clean walking across the casino carpet all night. People spill drinks constantly down there. Just last week, I was watching monitor four and this lady spilled a giant sticky margarita right across a blackjack table. The dealer looked like he wanted to cry. 

But, um... yeah. The cooking. I thought it would be fun like it used to be, but I ended up just stopping halfway through and ordering a pizza because I just didn't care enough to finish. It's the same with my morning runs. I put the shoes on, but it just feels like a chore I'm forcing myself to do, so I just take them back off.

They use this frozen chum that just bakes in the sun on the deck. \n\nBut, uh, no, I haven't really found anything that I actually enjoy doing right now. Just kind of... existing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Worthless? No, not really. I mean, I don't feel guilty about anything either. I just... I don't know, I just show up and do my job. Well, I try to, anyway. It's hard to feel like you're doing anything important when you're just staring at monitors all day. \n\nEspecially since they upgraded the whole camera system last year. They put in these new 4K lenses, right? Supposed to be top of the line. But the glare from the chandeliers in the main lobby completely washes out half the screens. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it's been... I'd say about three weeks now that I've felt this way about everything. It all started right around the time the casino decided to completely reorganize our security operations. They brought in this consulting firm from Vegas, these guys in expensive suits who probably haven't worked a floor in their lives. They changed all my assignments. 

I used to have a really solid rotation, you know? I'd do two hours on the main floor cameras, an hour monitoring the high-roller rooms, and then some time in the back halls. It broke the shift up nicely. But these new guys, they decided we need to be "specialized." So now they have me staring at the same bank of slot machines for eight hours straight. It's mind-numbing. 

And the new software they installed? It's supposed to use AI to flag suspicious behavior, but it's constantly going off for no reason. Like, if someone scratches their nose too fast, the system thinks they're trying to rig a machine. The alarms in the surveillance room sound like a dying smoke detector. It's this high-pitched chirp that just drills into your skull. I actually asked the IT guy—his name is Kevin, nice kid, always wears these vintage band t-shirts—I asked him if he could turn the volume down. He said he couldn't because it's hardwired into the new system. Kevin's been trying to get them to upgrade our chairs, too. The ones we have are from like, 2005. The lumbar support is completely shot. 

But anyway... yeah, it's been about three weeks of just not caring about the stuff I usually like to do. Ever since they messed with my schedule.

I actually tried making it at home once, but I couldn't find the right kind of dill at the grocery store. They only had that dried stuff in the little plastic containers, and Nick says you have to use fresh dill or it just tastes like dust. \n\nBut, uh... yeah. I don't want to kill myself or anything. Just... wouldn't mind if I didn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've tried recently where you expected to feel some pleasure, but didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying a minute ago about the cooking and the guitar and stuff... I actually tried to force myself to make a nice dinner for us just this past Friday. I figured, you know, I used to really look forward to doing it, so maybe if I just start chopping vegetables, I'll enjoy it again. But I was just standing in the kitchen, and it just felt like this massive chore. I couldn't get myself to care at all about how it was going to turn out. \n\nI was trying to dice an onion, actually. And I was using this new knife sharpener I bought off Amazon because my old chef's knife couldn't even slice a tomato without completely squishing it. You know how frustrating that is? It ruins the whole sandwich. Anyway, the sharpener was supposed to be this heavy-duty tungsten carbide thing. The reviews said it was used by professional butchers, but the delivery guy left the package at the wrong apartment. He left it at 4B instead of 4D. \n\nThe guy in 4B is this weird dude who actually works at the casino with me. I'm usually up in the surveillance room watching the cameras, making sure nobody's capping bets or whatever, but he's a floor manager down in the pit. He always wears these incredibly shiny dress shoes. Like, mirror-finish. I have no idea how he keeps them that clean walking across the casino carpet all night. People spill drinks constantly down there. Just last week, I was watching monitor four and this lady spilled a giant sticky margarita right across a blackjack table. The dealer looked like he wanted to cry. \n\nBut, um... yeah. The cooking. I thought it would be fun like it used to be, but I ended up just stopping halfway through and ordering a pizza because I just didn't care enough to finish. It's the same with my morning runs. I put the shoes on, but it just feels like a chore I'm forcing myself to do, so I just take them back off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been... I'd say about three weeks now that I've felt this way about everything. It all started right around the time the casino decided to completely reorganize our security operations. They brought in this consulting firm from Vegas, these guys in expensive suits who probably haven't worked a floor in their lives. They changed all my assignments. \n\nI used to have a really solid rotation, you know? I'd do two hours on the main floor cameras, an hour monitoring the high-roller rooms, and then some time in the back halls. It broke the shift up nicely. But these new guys, they decided we need to be \"specialized.\" So now they have me staring at the same bank of slot machines for eight hours straight. It's mind-numbing. \n\nAnd the new software they installed? It's supposed to use AI to flag suspicious behavior, but it's constantly going off for no reason. Like, if someone scratches their nose too fast, the system thinks they're trying to rig a machine. The alarms in the surveillance room sound like a dying smoke detector. It's this high-pitched chirp that just drills into your skull. I actually asked the IT guy—his name is Kevin, nice kid, always wears these vintage band t-shirts—I asked him if he could turn the volume down. He said he couldn't because it's hardwired into the new system. Kevin's been trying to get them to upgrade our chairs, too. The ones we have are from like, 2005. The lumbar support is completely shot. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, it's been about three weeks of just not caring about the stuff I usually like to do. Ever since they messed with my schedule.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you lost any weight recently?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 39 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"I came in because for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite, and even foods I usually like after a shift just sit there while I force down a few bites.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Weight? Um... I haven't really stepped on a scale to check the exact number, but yeah, I think I've lost some. I actually came in because for the last few weeks, I've barely had an appetite at all. Even the foods I usually look forward to after a long shift, I just end up staring at them and forcing down a few bites before I give up. 

It's weird because I used to have this whole routine after my shift ended at 2 AM. There's this 24-hour diner right across from the casino. Not Nick's place, a different one. It's called The Silver Spoon, but everyone just calls it "Spoons." They have these massive booths with the red vinyl seats that are always patched up with duct tape. I'd always get their country fried steak with extra gravy. The waitress there, Brenda, she's been working the graveyard shift for like twenty years. She knows my order by heart. She doesn't even ask anymore, just brings me a coffee and puts the ticket in. 

Brenda's got this crazy laugh, it sounds like a car engine trying to turn over in the winter. She was telling me the other night that her son just bought a used Mustang, but the transmission is already slipping. I told her he should take it to my mechanic, Sal. Sal's shop is all the way on the other side of town, but he's the only honest mechanic left. He won't charge you for blinker fluid or whatever nonsense the dealership tries to pull. He's got this massive Rottweiler that just sleeps in the office all day. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, unless you're wearing a hat. For some reason, that dog absolutely hates hats. 

But, uh, yeah... the country fried steak. I went in there last week, and Brenda brought it out, steaming hot, gravy everywhere. And I just... I looked at it, took maybe two bites, and pushed the plate away. Just didn't want it. So yeah, I'm probably dropping some weight.

Just kind of... existing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Worthless? No, not really. He swears our crab legs are the best in the city, but honestly, I think they taste like rubber. I don't know how anyone eats them, they just sit under those heat lamps all night. \n\nBut, um... yeah. About three weeks of feeling like this, ever since they switched up my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Are there any things that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I've tried to think of something, but nothing really comes to mind right now. I mean, my wife keeps telling me I should try watching those old Westerns I used to like. We have this whole box set of John Wayne movies in the basement. \n\nMy father-in-law gave it to us before he moved down to Florida. He bought this little condo right on the water in Boca. Swears he's gonna spend his whole retirement fishing, but the guy gets seasick just looking at a boat, I swear. We went out on a charter with him once, a few years back, and he spent the whole four hours hanging over the railing. The captain felt so bad he gave us a discount, which was nice, but the smell of the bait... man, I can't even look at shrimp anymore without my stomach turning. They use this frozen chum that just bakes in the sun on the deck. \n\nBut, uh, no, I haven't really found anything that I actually enjoy doing right now. Just kind of... existing, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Worthless? No, not really. I mean, I don't feel guilty about anything either. I just... I don't know, I just show up and do my job. Well, I try to, anyway. It's hard to feel like you're doing anything important when you're just staring at monitors all day. \n\nEspecially since they upgraded the whole camera system last year. They put in these new 4K lenses, right? Supposed to be top of the line. But the glare from the chandeliers in the main lobby completely washes out half the screens. I told the IT guy, Kevin, about it. Kevin is a piece of work, let me tell you. He walks around with this giant ring of keys on his belt like he's the warden of a prison, but half the time he can't even remember his own passwords. We were locked out of the employee portal for two days last month because he spilled a Monster energy drink on the main server tower. Just fried the whole motherboard. He tried to blame it on a power surge, but everyone saw the sticky green puddle on the floor. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... no, I don't feel guilty or anything. Just tired, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: My sleep? No, not really. I'm sleeping about the same as I always do, I guess. I mean, I try to get my usual hours in. It's been a little loud during the day sometimes because the guy next door decided he's a mechanic all of a sudden. He bought this completely rusted-out '98 Honda Civic and he's been revving the engine in his driveway. Thinks he's gonna fix it up and flip it, but the thing doesn't even have a front bumper. \n\nHis wife actually came over to apologize about the noise on Sunday and brought us a plate of cookies. Snickerdoodles, I think? But man, they were hard as rocks. I didn't want to be rude to her, so I took them, but I ended up tossing a couple to that stray cat that hangs around our back porch. Even the cat wouldn't eat them. Just sniffed 'em and walked away. Smart cat, honestly. My wife calls him Barnaby because he's got these crazy long white whiskers that make him look like a grumpy old man. We bought some actual cat food for him, but he only likes the seafood flavor. If you put the chicken one out, he'll just sit there and stare at you through the sliding glass door until you change it. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, sleep. Yeah, no changes with my sleep. Just the same old routine.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Small pleasures... I don't know. Not really. I used to look forward to my morning runs, you know? And I'd play my guitar in the evenings, or make a nice dinner for us. But lately, I just... I keep skipping all of it. It all just feels like chores now, stuff I can't even get myself to care about. \n\nI actually haven't touched the guitar in weeks. It's just sitting there on the stand in the living room collecting dust. It's an acoustic, a Martin. My dad bought it for me when I graduated high school. He drove all the way out to this little vintage shop in Pennsylvania to get it. The guy who ran the shop was this old hippie who claimed he used to tune guitars for the Grateful Dead back in the seventies. My dad said the guy had hair down to his waist and smelled like patchouli and stale beer. He kept trying to sell my dad these handmade leather straps with peace signs carved into them. \n\nDad ended up buying one just to get out of there, but he never used it. It's still in the original plastic bag in my closet somewhere. I think my wife almost threw it out when we were spring cleaning last year. She gets on these kicks where she wants to throw everything away. She read this book by that Japanese lady about folding your socks and only keeping things that spark joy or whatever. Next thing I know, she's trying to throw out my favorite coffee mug just because it has a chip in the handle. I've had that mug for ten years. \n\nBut yeah, the running and the guitar and cooking... I just don't care about any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Thoughts of death? Um... I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything. I don't have a plan. But lately, I just... I keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up, you know? Just so I wouldn't have to keep pushing through all this everyday. \n\nIt reminds me of this time I had the flu a couple years ago. It was that really bad strain that was going around. My whole body ached, and I slept for like three days straight. My wife had to bring me soup in bed, but I couldn't even eat it. It was this chicken noodle soup from that diner down the street, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing? The \"E\" has been burnt out for months so it just says \"DIN R\". The owner is this Greek guy named Nick. He makes the best gyro in town, hands down. The tzatziki sauce is incredible. He told me once that the secret is grating the cucumber and squeezing all the water out before you mix it into the yogurt. If you don't squeeze the water out, it gets all runny and ruins the pita bread. Nobody likes soggy pita bread. I actually tried making it at home once, but I couldn't find the right kind of dill at the grocery store. They only had that dried stuff in the little plastic containers, and Nick says you have to use fresh dill or it just tastes like dust. \n\nBut, uh... yeah. I don't want to kill myself or anything. Just... wouldn't mind if I didn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've tried recently where you expected to feel some pleasure, but didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying a minute ago about the cooking and the guitar and stuff... I actually tried to force myself to make a nice dinner for us just this past Friday. I figured, you know, I used to really look forward to doing it, so maybe if I just start chopping vegetables, I'll enjoy it again. But I was just standing in the kitchen, and it just felt like this massive chore. I couldn't get myself to care at all about how it was going to turn out. \n\nI was trying to dice an onion, actually. And I was using this new knife sharpener I bought off Amazon because my old chef's knife couldn't even slice a tomato without completely squishing it. You know how frustrating that is? It ruins the whole sandwich. Anyway, the sharpener was supposed to be this heavy-duty tungsten carbide thing. The reviews said it was used by professional butchers, but the delivery guy left the package at the wrong apartment. He left it at 4B instead of 4D. \n\nThe guy in 4B is this weird dude who actually works at the casino with me. I'm usually up in the surveillance room watching the cameras, making sure nobody's capping bets or whatever, but he's a floor manager down in the pit. He always wears these incredibly shiny dress shoes. Like, mirror-finish. I have no idea how he keeps them that clean walking across the casino carpet all night. People spill drinks constantly down there. Just last week, I was watching monitor four and this lady spilled a giant sticky margarita right across a blackjack table. The dealer looked like he wanted to cry. \n\nBut, um... yeah. The cooking. I thought it would be fun like it used to be, but I ended up just stopping halfway through and ordering a pizza because I just didn't care enough to finish. It's the same with my morning runs. I put the shoes on, but it just feels like a chore I'm forcing myself to do, so I just take them back off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been... I'd say about three weeks now that I've felt this way about everything. It all started right around the time the casino decided to completely reorganize our security operations. They brought in this consulting firm from Vegas, these guys in expensive suits who probably haven't worked a floor in their lives. They changed all my assignments. \n\nI used to have a really solid rotation, you know? I'd do two hours on the main floor cameras, an hour monitoring the high-roller rooms, and then some time in the back halls. It broke the shift up nicely. But these new guys, they decided we need to be \"specialized.\" So now they have me staring at the same bank of slot machines for eight hours straight. It's mind-numbing. \n\nAnd the new software they installed? It's supposed to use AI to flag suspicious behavior, but it's constantly going off for no reason. Like, if someone scratches their nose too fast, the system thinks they're trying to rig a machine. The alarms in the surveillance room sound like a dying smoke detector. It's this high-pitched chirp that just drills into your skull. I actually asked the IT guy—his name is Kevin, nice kid, always wears these vintage band t-shirts—I asked him if he could turn the volume down. He said he couldn't because it's hardwired into the new system. Kevin's been trying to get them to upgrade our chairs, too. The ones we have are from like, 2005. The lumbar support is completely shot. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, it's been about three weeks of just not caring about the stuff I usually like to do. Ever since they messed with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I haven't really stepped on a scale to check the exact number, but yeah, I think I've lost some. I actually came in because for the last few weeks, I've barely had an appetite at all. Even the foods I usually look forward to after a long shift, I just end up staring at them and forcing down a few bites before I give up. \n\nIt's weird because I used to have this whole routine after my shift ended at 2 AM. There's this 24-hour diner right across from the casino. Not Nick's place, a different one. It's called The Silver Spoon, but everyone just calls it \"Spoons.\" They have these massive booths with the red vinyl seats that are always patched up with duct tape. I'd always get their country fried steak with extra gravy. The waitress there, Brenda, she's been working the graveyard shift for like twenty years. She knows my order by heart. She doesn't even ask anymore, just brings me a coffee and puts the ticket in. \n\nBrenda's got this crazy laugh, it sounds like a car engine trying to turn over in the winter. She was telling me the other night that her son just bought a used Mustang, but the transmission is already slipping. I told her he should take it to my mechanic, Sal. Sal's shop is all the way on the other side of town, but he's the only honest mechanic left. He won't charge you for blinker fluid or whatever nonsense the dealership tries to pull. He's got this massive Rottweiler that just sleeps in the office all day. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, unless you're wearing a hat. For some reason, that dog absolutely hates hats. \n\nBut, uh, yeah... the country fried steak. I went in there last week, and Brenda brought it out, steaming hot, gravy everywhere. And I just... I looked at it, took maybe two bites, and pushed the plate away. Just didn't want it. So yeah, I'm probably dropping some weight.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely sluggish. It's funny you ask that, because people at work have actually been pointing it out recently. Like during our pre-shift briefings, they've noticed I'm just moving a lot slower than usual. And when someone asks me something, it's taking me way longer to answer. It just feels like my body and my mouth are a completely step behind everybody else right now. 

Which is annoying because Stan—he's our shift supervisor—he's already impatient enough as it is. He runs those briefings in this new conference room they moved us to down in the basement. It used to be a storage closet for broken slot machine parts, so it still smells like old machine grease and ozone. And the lighting is terrible, just these flickering fluorescent tubes that make everyone look gray. 

Plus, Stan insists on using this ancient overhead projector instead of just hooking his laptop up to the TV like a normal person. He prints out his notes on these transparent plastic sheets... I didn't even know they still made those things. He told me he buys them in bulk from this liquidator store out on Route 9, right next to the bowling alley. I used to bowl in a Thursday night league there, actually. That was back before they changed the oil patterns on the lanes. They started using this heavy synthetic oil, and my hook just completely died. I spent like two hundred bucks on a new reactive resin ball, but it didn't even help. The guy at the pro shop—this older guy with a massive handlebar mustache—he tried to tell me my release was wrong. But I've been throwing the ball the exact same way since I was fifteen, you know? Why would I change my grip just because they bought cheap lane oil?

Ever since they messed with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... Thinks he's gonna fix it up and flip it, but the thing doesn't even have a front bumper. \n\nHis wife actually came over to apologize about the noise on Sunday and brought us a plate of cookies. Snickerdoodles, I think? But man, they were hard as rocks. I didn't want to be rude to her, so I took them, but I ended up tossing a couple to that stray cat that hangs around our back porch. Even the cat wouldn't eat them. Just sniffed 'em and walked away. Smart cat, honestly. My wife calls him Barnaby because he's got these crazy long white whiskers that make him look like a grumpy old man. We bought some actual cat food for him, but he only likes the seafood flavor. If you put the chicken one out, he'll just sit there and stare at you through the sliding glass door until you change it. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, sleep. Yeah, no changes with my sleep. Just the same old routine.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Small pleasures... I don't know. Not really. I used to look forward to my morning runs, you know? And I'd play my guitar in the evenings, or make a nice dinner for us. But lately, I just... I keep skipping all of it. It all just feels like chores now, stuff I can't even get myself to care about. \n\nI actually haven't touched the guitar in weeks. It's just sitting there on the stand in the living room collecting dust. It's an acoustic, a Martin. My dad bought it for me when I graduated high school. He drove all the way out to this little vintage shop in Pennsylvania to get it. The guy who ran the shop was this old hippie who claimed he used to tune guitars for the Grateful Dead back in the seventies. My dad said the guy had hair down to his waist and smelled like patchouli and stale beer. He kept trying to sell my dad these handmade leather straps with peace signs carved into them. \n\nDad ended up buying one just to get out of there, but he never used it. It's still in the original plastic bag in my closet somewhere. I think my wife almost threw it out when we were spring cleaning last year. She gets on these kicks where she wants to throw everything away. She read this book by that Japanese lady about folding your socks and only keeping things that spark joy or whatever. Next thing I know, she's trying to throw out my favorite coffee mug just because it has a chip in the handle. I've had that mug for ten years. \n\nBut yeah, the running and the guitar and cooking... I just don't care about any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Thoughts of death? Um... I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything. I don't have a plan. But lately, I just... I keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up, you know? Just so I wouldn't have to keep pushing through all this everyday. \n\nIt reminds me of this time I had the flu a couple years ago. It was that really bad strain that was going around. My whole body ached, and I slept for like three days straight. My wife had to bring me soup in bed, but I couldn't even eat it. It was this chicken noodle soup from that diner down the street, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing? The \"E\" has been burnt out for months so it just says \"DIN R\". The owner is this Greek guy named Nick. He makes the best gyro in town, hands down. The tzatziki sauce is incredible. He told me once that the secret is grating the cucumber and squeezing all the water out before you mix it into the yogurt. If you don't squeeze the water out, it gets all runny and ruins the pita bread. Nobody likes soggy pita bread. I actually tried making it at home once, but I couldn't find the right kind of dill at the grocery store. They only had that dried stuff in the little plastic containers, and Nick says you have to use fresh dill or it just tastes like dust. \n\nBut, uh... yeah. I don't want to kill myself or anything. Just... wouldn't mind if I didn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've tried recently where you expected to feel some pleasure, but didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying a minute ago about the cooking and the guitar and stuff... I actually tried to force myself to make a nice dinner for us just this past Friday. I figured, you know, I used to really look forward to doing it, so maybe if I just start chopping vegetables, I'll enjoy it again. But I was just standing in the kitchen, and it just felt like this massive chore. I couldn't get myself to care at all about how it was going to turn out. \n\nI was trying to dice an onion, actually. And I was using this new knife sharpener I bought off Amazon because my old chef's knife couldn't even slice a tomato without completely squishing it. You know how frustrating that is? It ruins the whole sandwich. Anyway, the sharpener was supposed to be this heavy-duty tungsten carbide thing. The reviews said it was used by professional butchers, but the delivery guy left the package at the wrong apartment. He left it at 4B instead of 4D. \n\nThe guy in 4B is this weird dude who actually works at the casino with me. I'm usually up in the surveillance room watching the cameras, making sure nobody's capping bets or whatever, but he's a floor manager down in the pit. He always wears these incredibly shiny dress shoes. Like, mirror-finish. I have no idea how he keeps them that clean walking across the casino carpet all night. People spill drinks constantly down there. Just last week, I was watching monitor four and this lady spilled a giant sticky margarita right across a blackjack table. The dealer looked like he wanted to cry. \n\nBut, um... yeah. The cooking. I thought it would be fun like it used to be, but I ended up just stopping halfway through and ordering a pizza because I just didn't care enough to finish. It's the same with my morning runs. I put the shoes on, but it just feels like a chore I'm forcing myself to do, so I just take them back off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been... I'd say about three weeks now that I've felt this way about everything. It all started right around the time the casino decided to completely reorganize our security operations. They brought in this consulting firm from Vegas, these guys in expensive suits who probably haven't worked a floor in their lives. They changed all my assignments. \n\nI used to have a really solid rotation, you know? I'd do two hours on the main floor cameras, an hour monitoring the high-roller rooms, and then some time in the back halls. It broke the shift up nicely. But these new guys, they decided we need to be \"specialized.\" So now they have me staring at the same bank of slot machines for eight hours straight. It's mind-numbing. \n\nAnd the new software they installed? It's supposed to use AI to flag suspicious behavior, but it's constantly going off for no reason. Like, if someone scratches their nose too fast, the system thinks they're trying to rig a machine. The alarms in the surveillance room sound like a dying smoke detector. It's this high-pitched chirp that just drills into your skull. I actually asked the IT guy—his name is Kevin, nice kid, always wears these vintage band t-shirts—I asked him if he could turn the volume down. He said he couldn't because it's hardwired into the new system. Kevin's been trying to get them to upgrade our chairs, too. The ones we have are from like, 2005. The lumbar support is completely shot. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, it's been about three weeks of just not caring about the stuff I usually like to do. Ever since they messed with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I haven't really stepped on a scale to check the exact number, but yeah, I think I've lost some. I actually came in because for the last few weeks, I've barely had an appetite at all. Even the foods I usually look forward to after a long shift, I just end up staring at them and forcing down a few bites before I give up. \n\nIt's weird because I used to have this whole routine after my shift ended at 2 AM. There's this 24-hour diner right across from the casino. Not Nick's place, a different one. It's called The Silver Spoon, but everyone just calls it \"Spoons.\" They have these massive booths with the red vinyl seats that are always patched up with duct tape. I'd always get their country fried steak with extra gravy. The waitress there, Brenda, she's been working the graveyard shift for like twenty years. She knows my order by heart. She doesn't even ask anymore, just brings me a coffee and puts the ticket in. \n\nBrenda's got this crazy laugh, it sounds like a car engine trying to turn over in the winter. She was telling me the other night that her son just bought a used Mustang, but the transmission is already slipping. I told her he should take it to my mechanic, Sal. Sal's shop is all the way on the other side of town, but he's the only honest mechanic left. He won't charge you for blinker fluid or whatever nonsense the dealership tries to pull. He's got this massive Rottweiler that just sleeps in the office all day. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, unless you're wearing a hat. For some reason, that dog absolutely hates hats. \n\nBut, uh, yeah... the country fried steak. I went in there last week, and Brenda brought it out, steaming hot, gravy everywhere. And I just... I looked at it, took maybe two bites, and pushed the plate away. Just didn't want it. So yeah, I'm probably dropping some weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely sluggish. It's funny you ask that, because people at work have actually been pointing it out recently. Like during our pre-shift briefings, they've noticed I'm just moving a lot slower than usual. And when someone asks me something, it's taking me way longer to answer. It just feels like my body and my mouth are a completely step behind everybody else right now. \n\nWhich is annoying because Stan—he's our shift supervisor—he's already impatient enough as it is. He runs those briefings in this new conference room they moved us to down in the basement. It used to be a storage closet for broken slot machine parts, so it still smells like old machine grease and ozone. And the lighting is terrible, just these flickering fluorescent tubes that make everyone look gray. \n\nPlus, Stan insists on using this ancient overhead projector instead of just hooking his laptop up to the TV like a normal person. He prints out his notes on these transparent plastic sheets... I didn't even know they still made those things. He told me he buys them in bulk from this liquidator store out on Route 9, right next to the bowling alley. I used to bowl in a Thursday night league there, actually. That was back before they changed the oil patterns on the lanes. They started using this heavy synthetic oil, and my hook just completely died. I spent like two hundred bucks on a new reactive resin ball, but it didn't even help. The guy at the pro shop—this older guy with a massive handlebar mustache—he tried to tell me my release was wrong. But I've been throwing the ball the exact same way since I was fifteen, you know? Why would I change my grip just because they bought cheap lane oil?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, focusing has been... it's been really rough lately. Like, when I’m watching the CCTV feeds or trying to write up my incident reports at the end of the shift, my mind just completely drifts. I'll catch myself staring at the monitors but not actually seeing anything, or I'll have to reread the same line on a report over and over again just to understand what I just wrote. It's bad, honestly, which is how I ended up making a few dumb mistakes on the logs recently that I normally wouldn’t make. 

It's especially annoying with the incident reports because management still makes us use those old carbon-copy triplicate forms. You know the ones? Where you have to press down super hard so it bleeds through to the pink and yellow sheets. You can't use a gel pen on those, it just smudges everywhere. I had to go down to the main hotel lobby last week just to borrow a cheap ballpoint from Maria at the front desk. 

Maria's great, she always has a stash of pens she hides from the managers. She actually collects those little porcelain thimbles, too. Has hundreds of them. She was showing me pictures on her phone of this new one she got from Holland—it had a tiny blue windmill painted on it. Her daughter sent it to her. Her daughter moved out to Amsterdam a few years ago to work for some bicycle manufacturing company. I guess over there, everybody rides bikes everywhere. Even in the snow. I can't imagine riding a bike in the snow, my knees are way too shot for that. I tweaked my left knee playing softball back in high school, sliding into second base, and it still clicks every time it rains. The doctor back then told me I needed to ice it more, but ice just makes it stiff... 

But, uh, yeah. What were you asking? Right, concentrating. My brain just keeps wandering off like that when I'm trying to work.

He always wears these incredibly shiny dress shoes. Like, mirror-finish. I have no idea how he keeps them that clean walking across the casino carpet all night. People spill drinks constantly down there. Just last week, I was watching monitor four and this lady spilled a giant sticky margarita right across a blackjack table. The dealer looked like he wanted to cry. \n\nBut, um... yeah. The cooking. I thought it would be fun like it used to be, but I ended up just stopping halfway through and ordering a pizza because I just didn't care enough to finish. It's the same with my morning runs. I put the shoes on, but it just feels like a chore I'm forcing myself to do, so I just take them back off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been... I'd say about three weeks now that I've felt this way about everything. It all started right around the time the casino decided to completely reorganize our security operations. They brought in this consulting firm from Vegas, these guys in expensive suits who probably haven't worked a floor in their lives. I'd do two hours on the main floor cameras, an hour monitoring the high-roller rooms, and then some time in the back halls. It broke the shift up nicely. But these new guys, they decided we need to be \"specialized.\" So now they have me staring at the same bank of slot machines for eight hours straight. It's mind-numbing. \n\nAnd the new software they installed? It's supposed to use AI to flag suspicious behavior, but it's constantly going off for no reason. Like, if someone scratches their nose too fast, the system thinks they're trying to rig a machine. The alarms in the surveillance room sound like a dying smoke detector. It's this high-pitched chirp that just drills into your skull. I actually asked the IT guy—his name is Kevin, nice kid, always wears these vintage band t-shirts—I asked him if he could turn the volume down. He said he couldn't because it's hardwired into the new system. Kevin's been trying to get them to upgrade our chairs, too. The ones we have are from like, 2005. The lumbar support is completely shot. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, it's been about three weeks of just not caring about the stuff I usually like to do. Ever since they messed with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I haven't really stepped on a scale to check the exact number, but yeah, I think I've lost some. I actually came in because for the last few weeks, I've barely had an appetite at all. Even the foods I usually look forward to after a long shift, I just end up staring at them and forcing down a few bites before I give up. \n\nIt's weird because I used to have this whole routine after my shift ended at 2 AM. There's this 24-hour diner right across from the casino. Not Nick's place, a different one. It's called The Silver Spoon, but everyone just calls it \"Spoons.\" They have these massive booths with the red vinyl seats that are always patched up with duct tape. I'd always get their country fried steak with extra gravy. The waitress there, Brenda, she's been working the graveyard shift for like twenty years. She knows my order by heart. She doesn't even ask anymore, just brings me a coffee and puts the ticket in. \n\nBrenda's got this crazy laugh, it sounds like a car engine trying to turn over in the winter. She was telling me the other night that her son just bought a used Mustang, but the transmission is already slipping. I told her he should take it to my mechanic, Sal. Sal's shop is all the way on the other side of town, but he's the only honest mechanic left. He won't charge you for blinker fluid or whatever nonsense the dealership tries to pull. He's got this massive Rottweiler that just sleeps in the office all day. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, unless you're wearing a hat. For some reason, that dog absolutely hates hats. \n\nBut, uh, yeah... the country fried steak. I went in there last week, and Brenda brought it out, steaming hot, gravy everywhere. And I just... I looked at it, took maybe two bites, and pushed the plate away. Just didn't want it. So yeah, I'm probably dropping some weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely sluggish. It's funny you ask that, because people at work have actually been pointing it out recently. Like during our pre-shift briefings, they've noticed I'm just moving a lot slower than usual. And when someone asks me something, it's taking me way longer to answer. It just feels like my body and my mouth are a completely step behind everybody else right now. \n\nWhich is annoying because Stan—he's our shift supervisor—he's already impatient enough as it is. He runs those briefings in this new conference room they moved us to down in the basement. It used to be a storage closet for broken slot machine parts, so it still smells like old machine grease and ozone. And the lighting is terrible, just these flickering fluorescent tubes that make everyone look gray. \n\nPlus, Stan insists on using this ancient overhead projector instead of just hooking his laptop up to the TV like a normal person. He prints out his notes on these transparent plastic sheets... I didn't even know they still made those things. He told me he buys them in bulk from this liquidator store out on Route 9, right next to the bowling alley. I used to bowl in a Thursday night league there, actually. That was back before they changed the oil patterns on the lanes. They started using this heavy synthetic oil, and my hook just completely died. I spent like two hundred bucks on a new reactive resin ball, but it didn't even help. The guy at the pro shop—this older guy with a massive handlebar mustache—he tried to tell me my release was wrong. But I've been throwing the ball the exact same way since I was fifteen, you know? Why would I change my grip just because they bought cheap lane oil?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, focusing has been... it's been really rough lately. Like, when I’m watching the CCTV feeds or trying to write up my incident reports at the end of the shift, my mind just completely drifts. I'll catch myself staring at the monitors but not actually seeing anything, or I'll have to reread the same line on a report over and over again just to understand what I just wrote. It's bad, honestly, which is how I ended up making a few dumb mistakes on the logs recently that I normally wouldn’t make. \n\nIt's especially annoying with the incident reports because management still makes us use those old carbon-copy triplicate forms. You know the ones? Where you have to press down super hard so it bleeds through to the pink and yellow sheets. You can't use a gel pen on those, it just smudges everywhere. I had to go down to the main hotel lobby last week just to borrow a cheap ballpoint from Maria at the front desk. \n\nMaria's great, she always has a stash of pens she hides from the managers. She actually collects those little porcelain thimbles, too. Has hundreds of them. She was showing me pictures on her phone of this new one she got from Holland—it had a tiny blue windmill painted on it. Her daughter sent it to her. Her daughter moved out to Amsterdam a few years ago to work for some bicycle manufacturing company. I guess over there, everybody rides bikes everywhere. Even in the snow. I can't imagine riding a bike in the snow, my knees are way too shot for that. I tweaked my left knee playing softball back in high school, sliding into second base, and it still clicks every time it rains. The doctor back then told me I needed to ice it more, but ice just makes it stiff... \n\nBut, uh, yeah. What were you asking? Right, concentrating. My brain just keeps wandering off like that when I'm trying to work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The guy who ran the shop was this old hippie who claimed he used to tune guitars for the Grateful Dead back in the seventies. My dad said the guy had hair down to his waist and smelled like patchouli and stale beer. He kept trying to sell my dad these handmade leather straps with peace signs carved into them. \n\nDad ended up buying one just to get out of there, but he never used it. It's still in the original plastic bag in my closet somewhere. I think my wife almost threw it out when we were spring cleaning last year. She gets on these kicks where she wants to throw everything away. She read this book by that Japanese lady about folding your socks and only keeping things that spark joy or whatever. Next thing I know, she's trying to throw out my favorite coffee mug just because it has a chip in the handle. I've had that mug for ten years. \n\nBut yeah, the running and the guitar and cooking... I just don't care about any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Thoughts of death? Um... I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything. I don't have a plan. But lately, I just... I keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up, you know? Just so I wouldn't have to keep pushing through all this everyday. \n\nIt reminds me of this time I had the flu a couple years ago. It was that really bad strain that was going around. My whole body ached, and I slept for like three days straight. My wife had to bring me soup in bed, but I couldn't even eat it. It was this chicken noodle soup from that diner down the street, the one with the neon sign that's always buzzing? The \"E\" has been burnt out for months so it just says \"DIN R\". The owner is this Greek guy named Nick. He makes the best gyro in town, hands down. The tzatziki sauce is incredible. He told me once that the secret is grating the cucumber and squeezing all the water out before you mix it into the yogurt. If you don't squeeze the water out, it gets all runny and ruins the pita bread. Nobody likes soggy pita bread. I actually tried making it at home once, but I couldn't find the right kind of dill at the grocery store. They only had that dried stuff in the little plastic containers, and Nick says you have to use fresh dill or it just tastes like dust. \n\nBut, uh... yeah. I don't want to kill myself or anything. Just... wouldn't mind if I didn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've tried recently where you expected to feel some pleasure, but didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying a minute ago about the cooking and the guitar and stuff... I actually tried to force myself to make a nice dinner for us just this past Friday. I figured, you know, I used to really look forward to doing it, so maybe if I just start chopping vegetables, I'll enjoy it again. But I was just standing in the kitchen, and it just felt like this massive chore. I couldn't get myself to care at all about how it was going to turn out. \n\nI was trying to dice an onion, actually. And I was using this new knife sharpener I bought off Amazon because my old chef's knife couldn't even slice a tomato without completely squishing it. You know how frustrating that is? It ruins the whole sandwich. Anyway, the sharpener was supposed to be this heavy-duty tungsten carbide thing. The reviews said it was used by professional butchers, but the delivery guy left the package at the wrong apartment. He left it at 4B instead of 4D. \n\nThe guy in 4B is this weird dude who actually works at the casino with me. I'm usually up in the surveillance room watching the cameras, making sure nobody's capping bets or whatever, but he's a floor manager down in the pit. He always wears these incredibly shiny dress shoes. Like, mirror-finish. I have no idea how he keeps them that clean walking across the casino carpet all night. People spill drinks constantly down there. Just last week, I was watching monitor four and this lady spilled a giant sticky margarita right across a blackjack table. The dealer looked like he wanted to cry. \n\nBut, um... yeah. The cooking. I thought it would be fun like it used to be, but I ended up just stopping halfway through and ordering a pizza because I just didn't care enough to finish. It's the same with my morning runs. I put the shoes on, but it just feels like a chore I'm forcing myself to do, so I just take them back off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally find pleasurable for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been... I'd say about three weeks now that I've felt this way about everything. It all started right around the time the casino decided to completely reorganize our security operations. They brought in this consulting firm from Vegas, these guys in expensive suits who probably haven't worked a floor in their lives. They changed all my assignments. \n\nI used to have a really solid rotation, you know? I'd do two hours on the main floor cameras, an hour monitoring the high-roller rooms, and then some time in the back halls. It broke the shift up nicely. But these new guys, they decided we need to be \"specialized.\" So now they have me staring at the same bank of slot machines for eight hours straight. It's mind-numbing. \n\nAnd the new software they installed? It's supposed to use AI to flag suspicious behavior, but it's constantly going off for no reason. Like, if someone scratches their nose too fast, the system thinks they're trying to rig a machine. The alarms in the surveillance room sound like a dying smoke detector. It's this high-pitched chirp that just drills into your skull. I actually asked the IT guy—his name is Kevin, nice kid, always wears these vintage band t-shirts—I asked him if he could turn the volume down. He said he couldn't because it's hardwired into the new system. Kevin's been trying to get them to upgrade our chairs, too. The ones we have are from like, 2005. The lumbar support is completely shot. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, it's been about three weeks of just not caring about the stuff I usually like to do. Ever since they messed with my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I haven't really stepped on a scale to check the exact number, but yeah, I think I've lost some. I actually came in because for the last few weeks, I've barely had an appetite at all. Even the foods I usually look forward to after a long shift, I just end up staring at them and forcing down a few bites before I give up. \n\nIt's weird because I used to have this whole routine after my shift ended at 2 AM. There's this 24-hour diner right across from the casino. Not Nick's place, a different one. It's called The Silver Spoon, but everyone just calls it \"Spoons.\" They have these massive booths with the red vinyl seats that are always patched up with duct tape. I'd always get their country fried steak with extra gravy. The waitress there, Brenda, she's been working the graveyard shift for like twenty years. She knows my order by heart. She doesn't even ask anymore, just brings me a coffee and puts the ticket in. \n\nBrenda's got this crazy laugh, it sounds like a car engine trying to turn over in the winter. She was telling me the other night that her son just bought a used Mustang, but the transmission is already slipping. I told her he should take it to my mechanic, Sal. Sal's shop is all the way on the other side of town, but he's the only honest mechanic left. He won't charge you for blinker fluid or whatever nonsense the dealership tries to pull. He's got this massive Rottweiler that just sleeps in the office all day. Sweetest dog you'll ever meet, unless you're wearing a hat. For some reason, that dog absolutely hates hats. \n\nBut, uh, yeah... the country fried steak. I went in there last week, and Brenda brought it out, steaming hot, gravy everywhere. And I just... I looked at it, took maybe two bites, and pushed the plate away. Just didn't want it. So yeah, I'm probably dropping some weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or sluggish lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely sluggish. It's funny you ask that, because people at work have actually been pointing it out recently. Like during our pre-shift briefings, they've noticed I'm just moving a lot slower than usual. And when someone asks me something, it's taking me way longer to answer. It just feels like my body and my mouth are a completely step behind everybody else right now. \n\nWhich is annoying because Stan—he's our shift supervisor—he's already impatient enough as it is. He runs those briefings in this new conference room they moved us to down in the basement. It used to be a storage closet for broken slot machine parts, so it still smells like old machine grease and ozone. And the lighting is terrible, just these flickering fluorescent tubes that make everyone look gray. \n\nPlus, Stan insists on using this ancient overhead projector instead of just hooking his laptop up to the TV like a normal person. He prints out his notes on these transparent plastic sheets... I didn't even know they still made those things. He told me he buys them in bulk from this liquidator store out on Route 9, right next to the bowling alley. I used to bowl in a Thursday night league there, actually. That was back before they changed the oil patterns on the lanes. They started using this heavy synthetic oil, and my hook just completely died. I spent like two hundred bucks on a new reactive resin ball, but it didn't even help. The guy at the pro shop—this older guy with a massive handlebar mustache—he tried to tell me my release was wrong. But I've been throwing the ball the exact same way since I was fifteen, you know? Why would I change my grip just because they bought cheap lane oil?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, focusing has been... it's been really rough lately. Like, when I’m watching the CCTV feeds or trying to write up my incident reports at the end of the shift, my mind just completely drifts. I'll catch myself staring at the monitors but not actually seeing anything, or I'll have to reread the same line on a report over and over again just to understand what I just wrote. It's bad, honestly, which is how I ended up making a few dumb mistakes on the logs recently that I normally wouldn’t make. \n\nIt's especially annoying with the incident reports because management still makes us use those old carbon-copy triplicate forms. You know the ones? Where you have to press down super hard so it bleeds through to the pink and yellow sheets. You can't use a gel pen on those, it just smudges everywhere. I had to go down to the main hotel lobby last week just to borrow a cheap ballpoint from Maria at the front desk. \n\nMaria's great, she always has a stash of pens she hides from the managers. She actually collects those little porcelain thimbles, too. Has hundreds of them. She was showing me pictures on her phone of this new one she got from Holland—it had a tiny blue windmill painted on it. Her daughter sent it to her. Her daughter moved out to Amsterdam a few years ago to work for some bicycle manufacturing company. I guess over there, everybody rides bikes everywhere. Even in the snow. I can't imagine riding a bike in the snow, my knees are way too shot for that. I tweaked my left knee playing softball back in high school, sliding into second base, and it still clicks every time it rains. The doctor back then told me I needed to ice it more, but ice just makes it stiff... \n\nBut, uh, yeah. What were you asking? Right, concentrating. My brain just keeps wandering off like that when I'm trying to work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No, no, I don't have a plan or anything like that. I'm not planning to hurt myself. But... I don't know, lately I just keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up tomorrow. Like, if I could just go to sleep and not have to keep pushing through all this heaviness every single day. It just sounds kind of peaceful, honestly. 

Speaking of sleeping, I bought this new mattress pad a couple of months ago. It's one of those cooling gel memory foam ones. They advertise it all the time on those late-night infomercials, the ones with the guy who used to be on that soap opera in the nineties. I can't remember his name, but he always wears these brightly colored sweater vests. Anyway, the commercial makes it look like you're sleeping on a literal cloud, but the thing is so thick that none of my fitted sheets stay on the bed anymore. Every morning I wake up and the corners have popped off, and I'm just tangled in the sheets. 

I went to that big department store out by the interstate to try and find deep-pocket sheets, but the parking lot was an absolute nightmare. They're repaving the whole thing, so there was only one entrance open, and this guy in a massive lifted truck was blocking the whole lane trying to back into a spot that was clearly too small for him. He had one of those obnoxious exhaust pipes that sounds like a jet engine taking off. I don't get why people do that to their trucks. My cousin Joey did that to his Chevy back in the day, and his neighbors actually started a petition to get him to fix it because it was waking up all the babies on the street. Joey ended up moving to Florida anyway, to work on airboats in the Everglades. He sends me pictures sometimes of these massive alligators just chilling on the side of the road. 

But, um... yeah, no plan to do anything. Just... wouldn't mind if I didn't wake up, I guess.

She actually collects those little porcelain thimbles, too. Has hundreds of them. Her daughter moved out to Amsterdam a few years ago to work for some bicycle manufacturing company. I guess over there, everybody rides bikes everywhere. Even in the snow. I can't imagine riding a bike in the snow, my knees are way too shot for that. I tweaked my left knee playing softball back in high school, sliding into second base, and it still clicks every time it rains.
